#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Extract the design truth out of ``Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx``.

Deliverable C of the AI_Powered_PPT foundation. This is the *only* component that
is allowed to look inside the template; everything downstream (renderer, prompts,
AI author) consumes the two artifacts this script emits:

    reference/layout_manifest.json   machine contract  (renderer + validators read this)
    reference/layout_catalog.md      prompt context    (pasted into the AI author prompt)

Why both: the renderer needs exact EMU geometry, the LLM needs a short readable menu
of layouts with *content budgets* so it self-limits instead of overflowing boxes.
python-pptx cannot autofit text (PowerPoint computes autofit at render time, not in
the file), so the budget is our only pre-emptive defence against overflow.

Read-only by construction
-------------------------
The template is slurped into memory (``Path.read_bytes``) and handed to python-pptx
as a ``BytesIO``. python-pptx therefore never holds a handle on the real file and
``Presentation.save`` is never called. The ``&`` in ``Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx``
is a shell metacharacter, not a Python one -- because we never shell out, it needs no
escaping here. (Quote the path when *you* type it into cmd/PowerShell.)

Usage
-----
    python -m aippt.analyze_pptx_master
    python -m aippt.analyze_pptx_master --template "../Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx"
    python -m aippt.analyze_pptx_master --template <path> --out-dir <dir> --quiet

Exit codes: 0 ok | 2 template not found | 3 python-pptx missing | 4 parse failure.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import io
import json
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

# [REDACTED]
# Constants
# [REDACTED]

EMU_PER_INCH = 914400
EMU_PER_PT = 12700

#: DrawingML / PresentationML namespaces, in lxml Clark notation.
A = "{http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/drawingml/2006/main}"
P = "{http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/presentationml/2006/main}"

#: The Vieworks brand red. We do not assume where it lives -- we go and find it.
BRAND_ACCENT = "DB0032"

#: Ordered theme colour slots as they appear in ``a:clrScheme``.
CLR_SLOTS = (
    "dk1", "lt1", "dk2", "lt2",
    "accent1", "accent2", "accent3", "accent4", "accent5", "accent6",
    "hlink", "folHlink",
)

#: Fallback point sizes when neither the layout, the master placeholder, nor the
#: master ``p:txStyles`` declare one. Only used as a last resort; the manifest records
#: ``font_pt_source`` so you can tell a real value from a guess.
DEFAULT_PT = {"title": 36.0, "body": 18.0, "other": 12.0}

#: Typographic constants for the content-budget model. These are deliberately
#: conservative -- a budget that is slightly too small costs a word, a budget that is
#: too large costs a broken slide.
LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO = 0.52   # mean glyph advance / font size, proportional Latin
CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO = 1.00     # Hangul/Han are full-width by definition
LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO = 1.22        # single line spacing incl. leading
BULLET_GAP_RATIO = 0.35         # space-before between bullets, in font-size units
SAFETY = 0.90                   # trim 10% -- kerning, bold runs, and CJK/Latin mixing
LATIN_CHARS_PER_WORD = 6.0      # 5 letters + 1 space


# [REDACTED]
# Small helpers
# [REDACTED]

def emu_to_in(emu: int | None) -> float | None:
    return None if emu is None else round(emu / EMU_PER_INCH, 4)


def emu_to_pt(emu: int | None) -> float | None:
    return None if emu is None else round(emu / EMU_PER_PT, 2)


def aspect_label(w: int, h: int) -> str:
    """Name the aspect ratio, tolerating the rounding real templates contain."""
    if not w or not h:
        return "unknown"
    r = w / h
    for label, target in (("16:9", 16 / 9), ("16:10", 16 / 10), ("4:3", 4 / 3), ("3:2", 3 / 2)):
        if abs(r - target) < 0.02:
            return label
    return f"{r:.3f}:1"


def _rels_of(part: Any) -> list[Any]:
    """python-pptx has changed the Relationships type across versions; cope with both."""
    rels = part.rels
    try:
        return list(rels.values())
    except AttributeError:
        return [rels[k] for k in rels]


def _element_of(obj: Any) -> Any:
    """Raw lxml element behind a python-pptx object (private API, hence the fallback)."""
    for attr in ("_element", "element"):
        el = getattr(obj, attr, None)
        if el is not None:
            return el
    raise AttributeError(f"cannot reach the XML element of {obj!r}")


# [REDACTED]
# Theme
# [REDACTED]

@dataclass
class ThemeInfo:
    name: str | None = None
    major_font: dict[str, str | None] = field(default_factory=dict)
    minor_font: dict[str, str | None] = field(default_factory=dict)
    color_map: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
    brand_accent_slots: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)


def _typefaces(font_el: Any) -> dict[str, str | None]:
    """latin / ea (East Asian -- the Korean face) / cs typefaces of a font scheme half."""
    if font_el is None:
        return {}
    out: dict[str, str | None] = {}
    for key in ("latin", "ea", "cs"):
        el = font_el.find(f"{A}{key}")
        out[key] = el.get("typeface") if el is not None else None
    return out


def _slot_color(slot_el: Any) -> str | None:
    """Resolve one ``a:clrScheme`` slot to an RRGGBB string."""
    if slot_el is None:
        return None
    srgb = slot_el.find(f"{A}srgbClr")
    if srgb is not None and srgb.get("val"):
        return srgb.get("val").upper()
    sysclr = slot_el.find(f"{A}sysClr")
    if sysclr is not None:
        # sysClr carries the resolved value in @lastClr; @val is a system name (e.g. windowText)
        return (sysclr.get("lastClr") or sysclr.get("val") or "").upper() or None
    return None


def read_theme(master: Any) -> ThemeInfo:
    """Pull the font scheme + colour map off the theme part related to this master.

    python-pptx exposes no theme API, so we walk the OPC relationships by hand.
    Returns an empty-ish ThemeInfo rather than raising if the theme is unreachable --
    a missing theme must not sink the whole analysis.
    """
    info = ThemeInfo()
    theme_el = None
    for rel in _rels_of(master.part):
        if str(rel.reltype).endswith("/theme"):
            try:
                from lxml import etree
                theme_el = etree.fromstring(rel.target_part.blob)
            except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
                theme_el = None
            break
    if theme_el is None:
        return info

    info.name = theme_el.get("name")
    scheme = theme_el.find(f"{A}themeElements")
    if scheme is None:
        return info

    font_scheme = scheme.find(f"{A}fontScheme")
    if font_scheme is not None:
        info.major_font = _typefaces(font_scheme.find(f"{A}majorFont"))
        info.minor_font = _typefaces(font_scheme.find(f"{A}minorFont"))

    clr_scheme = scheme.find(f"{A}clrScheme")
    if clr_scheme is not None:
        for slot in CLR_SLOTS:
            value = _slot_color(clr_scheme.find(f"{A}{slot}"))
            if value:
                info.color_map[slot] = value
                if value == BRAND_ACCENT:
                    info.brand_accent_slots.append(slot)
    return info


def find_brand_accent(master: Any, theme: ThemeInfo) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Answer the brief's question: *where does #DB0032 actually live?*

    Checks, in order of authority:
      1. the theme colour scheme (the answer we want -- means the renderer can just say
         ``MSO_THEME_COLOR.ACCENT_n`` and inherit the brand automatically);
      2. hard-coded ``srgbClr val="DB0032"`` anywhere in the master's own XML.
    """
    hits = {
        "theme_slots": list(theme.brand_accent_slots),
        "hardcoded_in_master_xml": 0,
        "resolution": "not-found",
    }
    try:
        master_el = _element_of(master)
        for el in master_el.iter(f"{A}srgbClr"):
            if (el.get("val") or "").upper() == BRAND_ACCENT:
                hits["hardcoded_in_master_xml"] += 1
    except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
        pass

    if hits["theme_slots"]:
        slots = ", ".join(hits["theme_slots"])
        hits["resolution"] = (
            f"theme colour slot(s): {slots} -- renderer should reference the theme slot, "
            f"never the literal hex, so a future rebrand stays a one-file change"
        )
    elif hits["hardcoded_in_master_xml"]:
        hits["resolution"] = (
            f"NOT in the theme; hard-coded {hits['hardcoded_in_master_xml']}x in the master XML. "
            f"Renderer must use the literal RGBColor(0xDB, 0x00, 0x32)."
        )
    return hits


# [REDACTED]
# Font size resolution
# [REDACTED]

TITLE_TYPES = {"TITLE", "CENTER_TITLE", "VERTICAL_TITLE"}
BODY_TYPES = {"BODY", "SUBTITLE", "OBJECT", "VERTICAL_BODY", "VERTICAL_OBJECT"}


def _lvl1_size_pt(el: Any) -> float | None:
    """First ``a:lvl1pPr/a:defRPr@sz`` under ``el``. ``sz`` is in 1/100 pt (4400 == 44pt)."""
    if el is None:
        return None
    for lvl in el.iter(f"{A}lvl1pPr"):
        rpr = lvl.find(f"{A}defRPr")
        if rpr is not None and rpr.get("sz"):
            try:
                return int(rpr.get("sz")) / 100.0
            except ValueError:
                return None
    return None


def _txstyles_size_pt(master: Any, ph_type: str) -> float | None:
    """Master-level ``p:txStyles`` default for this class of placeholder."""
    try:
        master_el = _element_of(master)
    except AttributeError:
        return None
    tx = master_el.find(f"{P}txStyles")
    if tx is None:
        return None
    if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
        style = tx.find(f"{P}titleStyle")
    elif ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
        style = tx.find(f"{P}bodyStyle")
    else:
        style = tx.find(f"{P}otherStyle")
    return _lvl1_size_pt(style)


def resolve_font_pt(layout_ph: Any, master_ph: Any, master: Any, ph_type: str) -> tuple[float, str]:
    """Effective lvl-1 font size for a placeholder, plus *where the number came from*.

    Inheritance chain, most specific first -- this mirrors what PowerPoint itself does:
    layout placeholder list-style -> matching master placeholder list-style ->
    master ``p:txStyles`` -> hard default.

    Returns ``(points, source)``. ``source`` is recorded in the manifest so a reader can
    distinguish a real template value from our fallback guess.
    """
    for obj, source in ((layout_ph, "layout"), (master_ph, "master-placeholder")):
        if obj is None:
            continue
        try:
            size = _lvl1_size_pt(_element_of(obj))
        except AttributeError:
            size = None
        if size:
            return size, source

    size = _txstyles_size_pt(master, ph_type)
    if size:
        return size, "master-txStyles"

    if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
        return DEFAULT_PT["title"], "default-guess"
    if ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
        return DEFAULT_PT["body"], "default-guess"
    return DEFAULT_PT["other"], "default-guess"


# [REDACTED]
# Content budget
# [REDACTED]

def content_budget(width_emu: int | None, height_emu: int | None, font_pt: float,
                   ph_type: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """How much text actually fits in this box -- the number we hand to the LLM.

    This is a *typographic estimate*, not a rendering. It exists because python-pptx
    cannot autofit and PowerPoint will happily let text spill off the slide. The model
    is deliberately simple and conservative:

        chars_per_line = box_width_pt / (font_pt * char_width_ratio) * SAFETY
        lines          = box_height_pt / (font_pt * LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO)

    Two character budgets are reported because this corpus is bilingual: a Hangul glyph
    is ~2x the advance width of a Latin one, so a box that holds 60 Latin characters
    holds only ~31 Korean ones. Mixed text should be budgeted against ``cjk``.

    Titles are reported as a flat character cap (they get 1-2 lines). Body placeholders
    are reported as ``max_bullets x max_words_per_bullet``, which is the shape the
    prompt actually needs.
    """
    if not width_emu or not height_emu or not font_pt:
        return {"computable": False,
                "reason": "placeholder has no resolved geometry or font size"}

    w_pt = width_emu / EMU_PER_PT
    h_pt = height_emu / EMU_PER_PT

    latin_cpl = max(1, int(w_pt / (font_pt * LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO) * SAFETY))
    cjk_cpl = max(1, int(w_pt / (font_pt * CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO) * SAFETY))
    max_lines = max(1, int(h_pt / (font_pt * LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO)))

    budget: dict[str, Any] = {
        "computable": True,
        "font_pt": font_pt,
        "chars_per_line_latin": latin_cpl,
        "chars_per_line_cjk": cjk_cpl,
        "max_lines": max_lines,
    }

    if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
        # A title that wraps to 3 lines is a design failure; cap the usable lines at 2.
        title_lines = min(2, max_lines)
        budget["max_title_chars_latin"] = latin_cpl * title_lines
        budget["max_title_chars_cjk"] = cjk_cpl * title_lines
        budget["recommended_lines"] = title_lines
    elif ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
        # Each bullet costs its own line plus the space-before gap.
        per_bullet = LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO + BULLET_GAP_RATIO
        max_bullets = max(1, int(h_pt / (font_pt * per_bullet)))
        budget["max_bullets"] = max_bullets
        budget["max_words_per_bullet"] = max(1, int(latin_cpl / LATIN_CHARS_PER_WORD))
        budget["max_chars_per_bullet_latin"] = latin_cpl
        budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"] = cjk_cpl
        budget["note"] = "one line per bullet; a bullet that wraps consumes another slot"
    return budget


# [REDACTED]
# Semantic role
# [REDACTED]

#: Layout names are authored by humans and this template is Korean; match both languages.
ROLE_NAME_HINTS = (
    ("section-divider", ("section", "divider", "chapter", "구분", "간지", "섹션")),
    ("agenda", ("agenda", "contents", "toc", "목차", "차례", "개요")),
    ("title", ("title slide", "제목", "표지", "cover")),
    ("closing", ("thank", "end", "closing", "감사", "마무리")),
    ("quote", ("quote", "인용")),
    ("comparison", ("comparison", "compare", "비교")),
)


def suggest_role(layout_name: str, ph_types: list[str]) -> str:
    """Best guess at what a layout is *for*, so the AI can pick one by intent.

    Name hints win when present (a human named it), otherwise fall back to placeholder
    composition. This is a suggestion, not a contract -- a human should sanity-check the
    catalog once and correct any row that is wrong.
    """
    lname = (layout_name or "").strip().lower()
    for role, needles in ROLE_NAME_HINTS:
        if any(n in lname for n in needles):
            return role

    content = [t for t in ph_types if t not in ("SLIDE_NUMBER", "FOOTER", "DATE", "HEADER")]
    titles = [t for t in content if t in TITLE_TYPES]
    bodies = [t for t in content if t in BODY_TYPES]

    if not content:
        return "blank"
    if "PICTURE" in content or "BITMAP" in content:
        return "picture"
    if "TABLE" in content:
        return "table"
    if "CHART" in content:
        return "chart"
    if "CENTER_TITLE" in content or "SUBTITLE" in content:
        return "title"
    if titles and not bodies:
        return "title-only"
    if titles and len(bodies) >= 3:
        return "multi-content"
    if titles and len(bodies) == 2:
        return "two-content"
    if titles and len(bodies) == 1:
        return "bullets"
    return "custom"


# [REDACTED]
# Extraction
# [REDACTED]

def _ph_type_name(ph: Any) -> str:
    try:
        t = ph.placeholder_format.type
    except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
        return "UNKNOWN"
    if t is None:
        return "UNKNOWN"
    # PP_PLACEHOLDER enum members stringify as e.g. "BODY (2)" depending on version.
    return getattr(t, "name", None) or str(t).split()[0]


def _master_ph_for(master: Any, idx: int, ph_type: str) -> Any:
    """The master placeholder a layout placeholder inherits from: match idx, then type."""
    candidates = list(master.placeholders)
    for ph in candidates:
        if ph.placeholder_format.idx == idx:
            return ph
    for ph in candidates:
        if _ph_type_name(ph) == ph_type:
            return ph
    return None


def extract_placeholder(layout_ph: Any, master: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """One placeholder -> its manifest row, geometry resolved and budget computed."""
    fmt = layout_ph.placeholder_format
    idx = fmt.idx
    ph_type = _ph_type_name(layout_ph)
    master_ph = _master_ph_for(master, idx, ph_type)

    # Geometry may be absent on the layout and inherited from the master. python-pptx
    # surfaces that as None rather than resolving it, so resolve it ourselves.
    geom: dict[str, int | None] = {}
    inherited = False
    for key in ("left", "top", "width", "height"):
        value = getattr(layout_ph, key, None)
        if value is None and master_ph is not None:
            value = getattr(master_ph, key, None)
            if value is not None:
                inherited = True
        geom[key] = None if value is None else int(value)

    font_pt, font_src = resolve_font_pt(layout_ph, master_ph, master, ph_type)

    return {
        "idx": idx,
        "type": ph_type,
        "name": layout_ph.name,
        "geometry_inherited_from_master": inherited,
        "emu": dict(geom),
        "inches": {k: emu_to_in(v) for k, v in geom.items()},
        "font_pt": font_pt,
        "font_pt_source": font_src,
        "content_budget": content_budget(geom["width"], geom["height"], font_pt, ph_type),
    }


def extract_layout(index: int, layout: Any, master: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
    placeholders = [extract_placeholder(ph, master) for ph in layout.placeholders]
    # A None idx would make a plain (is None, idx) key compare None < None -> TypeError.
    placeholders.sort(key=lambda p: (p["idx"] is None, p["idx"] if p["idx"] is not None else -1))
    ph_types = [p["type"] for p in placeholders]

    # Shapes the template itself draws (logo bars, rules). They render on every slide
    # using this layout and are NOT ours to fill -- but they DO eat visual space.
    decorations = [s.name for s in layout.shapes if not s.is_placeholder]

    return {
        "index": index,
        "layout_id": layout.name,
        "name": layout.name,
        "semantic_role": suggest_role(layout.name, ph_types),
        "placeholder_count": len(placeholders),
        "decoration_shapes": decorations,
        "placeholders": placeholders,
    }


def analyze(template: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Open the template read-only and return the full manifest dict."""
    from pptx import Presentation

    raw = template.read_bytes()
    digest = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
    # BytesIO => python-pptx never touches the file on disk. Read-only, guaranteed.
    prs = Presentation(io.BytesIO(raw))

    width, height = int(prs.slide_width), int(prs.slide_height)

    masters: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    for mi, master in enumerate(prs.slide_masters):
        theme = read_theme(master)
        masters.append({
            "index": mi,
            "name": master.name,
            "theme_name": theme.name,
            "font_scheme": {"major": theme.major_font, "minor": theme.minor_font},
            "color_map": theme.color_map,
            "brand_accent": find_brand_accent(master, theme),
            "layout_count": len(master.slide_layouts),
            "layouts": [extract_layout(li, lay, master)
                        for li, lay in enumerate(master.slide_layouts)],
        })

    existing = [{"index": i, "layout": s.slide_layout.name, "shape_count": len(s.shapes)}
                for i, s in enumerate(prs.slides)]

    return {
        "schema_version": "1.0",
        "generated_by": "aippt.analyze_pptx_master",
        "source": {
            "filename": template.name,
            "sha256": digest,
            "bytes": len(raw),
        },
        "deck": {
            "slide_width_emu": width,
            "slide_height_emu": height,
            "slide_width_in": emu_to_in(width),
            "slide_height_in": emu_to_in(height),
            "aspect_ratio": aspect_label(width, height),
            "master_count": len(prs.slide_masters),
            "layout_count_first_master": len(prs.slide_layouts),
            "existing_slide_count": len(prs.slides),
        },
        "masters": masters,
        "existing_slides": existing,
        "budget_model": {
            "latin_char_width_ratio": LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO,
            "cjk_char_width_ratio": CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO,
            "line_height_ratio": LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO,
            "safety_factor": SAFETY,
            "caveat": "typographic estimate, not a render; the render->PNG check is authoritative",
        },
    }


# [REDACTED]
# Rendering the artifacts
# [REDACTED]

def render_catalog(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """The prompt-context artifact: everything the AI author needs, nothing it doesn't.

    Kept deliberately compact -- this text is pasted into every generation call, so every
    token in it is paid for on every slide.
    """
    deck = manifest["deck"]
    out: list[str] = []
    w = out.append

    w("# Layout Catalog")
    w("")
    w(f"Generated from `{manifest['source']['filename']}` "
      f"(sha256 `{manifest['source']['sha256'][:12]}...`) by `analyze_pptx_master.py`.")
    w("**Do not hand-edit** -- re-run the analyzer instead.")
    w("")
    w(f"- Slide size: **{deck['slide_width_in']}in x {deck['slide_height_in']}in** "
      f"({deck['aspect_ratio']}), {deck['slide_width_emu']} x {deck['slide_height_emu']} EMU")
    w(f"- Masters: **{deck['master_count']}** | "
      f"Layouts (first master): **{deck['layout_count_first_master']}** | "
      f"Existing slides: **{deck['existing_slide_count']}**")
    w("")

    for master in manifest["masters"]:
        w(f"## Master {master['index']}: {master['name']}")
        w("")
        major = master["font_scheme"].get("major") or {}
        minor = master["font_scheme"].get("minor") or {}
        w(f"- Theme: `{master['theme_name']}`")
        w(f"- Major font (headings): latin `{major.get('latin')}` / east-asian `{major.get('ea')}`")
        w(f"- Minor font (body): latin `{minor.get('latin')}` / east-asian `{minor.get('ea')}`")
        w(f"- Brand accent `#{BRAND_ACCENT}`: {master['brand_accent']['resolution']}")
        if master["color_map"]:
            pairs = ", ".join(f"{k}=#{v}" for k, v in master["color_map"].items())
            w(f"- Colour map: {pairs}")
        w("")
        w("### Layouts")
        w("")
        w("| # | layout_id | role | placeholders |")
        w("|---|-----------|------|--------------|")
        for lay in master["layouts"]:
            types = ", ".join(f"{p['type']}(idx {p['idx']})" for p in lay["placeholders"]) or "-none-"
            w(f"| {lay['index']} | `{lay['layout_id']}` | {lay['semantic_role']} | {types} |")
        w("")

        w("### Content budgets")
        w("")
        w("The AI author MUST respect these. `cjk` applies to Korean text, `latin` to English;")
        w("for mixed text, budget against `cjk`.")
        w("")
        for lay in master["layouts"]:
            if not lay["placeholders"]:
                continue
            w(f"**`{lay['layout_id']}`** ({lay['semantic_role']})")
            w("")
            for ph in lay["placeholders"]:
                b = ph["content_budget"]
                head = f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph['type']} -- {ph['inches']['width']}x{ph['inches']['height']}in @ {ph['font_pt']}pt"
                if not b.get("computable"):
                    w(head + f" -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason')})")
                    continue
                if "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
                    w(head + f" -- max **{b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin chars** "
                             f"/ **{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars**, "
                             f"<= {b['recommended_lines']} lines")
                elif "max_bullets" in b:
                    w(head + f" -- max **{b['max_bullets']} bullets** x "
                             f"**{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words** "
                             f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
                             f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)")
                else:
                    w(head + f" -- {b['chars_per_line_latin']} latin chars/line, "
                             f"{b['max_lines']} lines")
            w("")

    w("---")
    w("")
    w("## Allowed `layout_id` values")
    w("")
    w("The IR `layout_id` field MUST be exactly one of:")
    w("")
    for master in manifest["masters"]:
        for lay in master["layouts"]:
            w(f"- `{lay['layout_id']}`  ({lay['semantic_role']})")
    w("")
    return "\n".join(out) + "\n"


def print_summary(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    deck = manifest["deck"]
    print("=" * 78)
    print(f"  {manifest['source']['filename']}")
    print("=" * 78)
    print(f"  slide size : {deck['slide_width_in']}in x {deck['slide_height_in']}in  "
          f"({deck['aspect_ratio']})  [{deck['slide_width_emu']} x {deck['slide_height_emu']} EMU]")
    print(f"  masters    : {deck['master_count']}")
    print(f"  layouts    : {deck['layout_count_first_master']} (first master)")
    print(f"  slides     : {deck['existing_slide_count']} already in the template")
    print()

    for master in manifest["masters"]:
        print(f"MASTER {master['index']}: {master['name']}  (theme: {master['theme_name']})")
        major = master["font_scheme"].get("major") or {}
        minor = master["font_scheme"].get("minor") or {}
        print(f"  fonts  : major latin={major.get('latin')} ea={major.get('ea')} | "
              f"minor latin={minor.get('latin')} ea={minor.get('ea')}")
        print(f"  #{BRAND_ACCENT}: {master['brand_accent']['resolution']}")
        if master["color_map"]:
            print("  colors : " + ", ".join(f"{k}=#{v}" for k, v in master["color_map"].items()))
        print()
        for lay in master["layouts"]:
            print(f"  [{lay['index']:>2}] {lay['layout_id']!r}  "
                  f"role={lay['semantic_role']}  placeholders={lay['placeholder_count']}")
            for ph in lay["placeholders"]:
                inch = ph["inches"]
                mark = " (inherited)" if ph["geometry_inherited_from_master"] else ""
                print(f"        idx={ph['idx']:<3} {ph['type']:<14} {ph['name']!r}")
                print(f"          box  : L={inch['left']} T={inch['top']} "
                      f"W={inch['width']} H={inch['height']} in{mark}")
                print(f"          font : {ph['font_pt']}pt  (from {ph['font_pt_source']})")
                b = ph["content_budget"]
                if not b.get("computable"):
                    print(f"          budget: n/a - {b.get('reason')}")
                elif "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
                    print(f"          budget: title <= {b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin "
                          f"/ {b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars")
                elif "max_bullets" in b:
                    print(f"          budget: {b['max_bullets']} bullets x "
                          f"{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words "
                          f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars/bullet)")
                else:
                    print(f"          budget: {b['chars_per_line_latin']} latin chars/line "
                          f"x {b['max_lines']} lines")
            if lay["decoration_shapes"]:
                print(f"        decoration (drawn by the template, not fillable): "
                      f"{', '.join(lay['decoration_shapes'])}")
            print()

    if manifest["existing_slides"]:
        print("EXISTING SLIDES IN TEMPLATE")
        for s in manifest["existing_slides"]:
            print(f"  [{s['index']:>2}] layout={s['layout']!r} shapes={s['shape_count']}")
        print()


# [REDACTED]
# CLI
# [REDACTED]

DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME = "Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx"


def find_default_template(start: Path) -> Path | None:
    """Look for the template beside the repo, then walk up. Keeps the no-arg case working."""
    for base in (start, *start.parents):
        candidate = base / DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME
        if candidate.is_file():
            return candidate
    return None


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    here = Path(__file__).resolve()
    repo_root = here.parents[2]          # .../src/aippt/x.py -> repo root

    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Extract layouts, theme and content budgets from the PPTX master.")
    parser.add_argument("--template", type=Path, default=None,
                        help=f"path to the .pptx (default: nearest {DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME})")
    parser.add_argument("--out-dir", type=Path, default=repo_root / "reference",
                        help="where to write layout_manifest.json / layout_catalog.md")
    parser.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true", help="suppress the stdout summary")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    # Korean layout names and Hangul in the summary must survive a Windows console.
    try:
        sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except (AttributeError, ValueError):  # pragma: no cover - very old / redirected stdout
        pass

    template = args.template or find_default_template(repo_root)
    if template is None:
        print(f"ERROR: could not find {DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME}. Pass --template <path>.",
              file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
    template = Path(template).expanduser().resolve()
    if not template.is_file():
        print(f"ERROR: template not found: {template}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2

    try:
        import pptx  # noqa: F401
    except ImportError:
        print("ERROR: python-pptx is not installed.  pip install -r requirements.txt",
              file=sys.stderr)
        return 3

    try:
        manifest = analyze(template)
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - top-level CLI guard, we want the message
        print(f"ERROR: failed to parse {template.name}: {exc!r}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 4

    out_dir: Path = args.out_dir
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    json_path = out_dir / "layout_manifest.json"
    md_path = out_dir / "layout_catalog.md"
    json_path.write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    md_path.write_text(render_catalog(manifest), encoding="utf-8")

    if not args.quiet:
        print_summary(manifest)
    print(f"wrote {json_path}")
    print(f"wrote {md_path}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command "@'
import json, posixpath, zipfile
from pathlib import Path
from lxml import etree

pptx_path = Path(r'C:\\Users\\2021670\\Documents\\멀티 에이전트\\tasks\\AI_Powered_PPT\\Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx')
NS = {
 'p':'http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/presentationml/2006/main',
 'a':'http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/drawingml/2006/main',
 'r':'http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships',
 'pr':'http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/package/2006/relationships',
}
def rels_path(part):
    d, f = posixpath.split(part)
    return posixpath.join(d, '_rels', f + '.rels')

def resolve(part, target):
    return posixpath.normpath(posixpath.join(posixpath.dirname(part), target))

def relmap(z, part):
    rp = rels_path(part)
    root = etree.fromstring(z.read(rp))
    return {x.get('Id'): {'type':x.get('Type','').rsplit('/',1)[-1], 'target':resolve(part,x.get('Target'))} for x in root}

def q1(el, xp):
    x = el.xpath(xp, namespaces=NS)
    return x[0] if x else None

def geom(el):
    off = q1(el, './/a:xfrm/a:off | .//p:xfrm/a:off')
    ext = q1(el, './/a:xfrm/a:ext | .//p:xfrm/a:ext')
    if off is None or ext is None:
        return None
    vals = [int(off.get('x')), int(off.get('y')), int(ext.get('cx')), int(ext.get('cy'))]
    return {'emu': vals, 'in': [round(v/914400,4) for v in vals], 'pt': [round(v/12700,2) for v in vals]}

def shape_row(el):
    tag = etree.QName(el).localname
    cnv = q1(el, './p:nvSpPr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvPicPr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvGraphicFramePr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvCxnSpPr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvGrpSpPr/p:cNvPr')
    ph = q1(el, './/p:nvPr/p:ph')
    blip = q1(el, './/a:blip')
    return {
      'tag': tag,
      'id': cnv.get('id') if cnv is not None else None,
      'name': cnv.get('name') if cnv is not None else None,
      'placeholder': None if ph is None else {'type':ph.get('type'), 'idx':ph.get('idx')},
      'geom': geom(el),
      'text': ''.join(el.xpath('.//a:t/text()', namespaces=NS)),
      'blip_rId': None if blip is None else blip.get('{%s}embed' % NS['r']),
    }

def top_shapes(root):
    sp_tree = q1(root, './p:cSld/p:spTree')
    return [shape_row(x) for x in sp_tree if etree.QName(x).localname not in ('nvGrpSpPr','grpSpPr')]

with zipfile.ZipFile(pptx_path) as z:
    pres_part='ppt/presentation.xml'
    pres=etree.fromstring(z.read(pres_part))
    pres_rels=relmap(z,pres_part)
    layout_ids=pres.xpath('./p:sldMasterIdLst/p:sldMasterId',namespaces=NS)
    master_part=pres_rels[layout_ids[0].get('{%s}id'%NS['r'])]['target']
    master=etree.fromstring(z.read(master_part))
    mrels=relmap(z,master_part)
    ordered_layout_parts=[]
    for x in master.xpath('./p:sldLayoutIdLst/p:sldLayoutId',namespaces=NS):
        ordered_layout_parts.append(mrels[x.get('{%s}id'%NS['r'])]['target'])
    layout_part=ordered_layout_parts[3]
    layout=etree.fromstring(z.read(layout_part))
    lrels=relmap(z,layout_part)
    slides=[]
    for i,sid in enumerate(pres.xpath('./p:sldIdLst/p:sldId',namespaces=NS),1):
        spart=pres_rels[sid.get('{%s}id'%NS['r'])]['target']
        srels=relmap(z,spart)
        lr=[v['target'] for v in srels.values() if v['type']=='slideLayout']
        if lr and lr[0]==layout_part:
            sroot=etree.fromstring(z.read(spart))
            slides.append({'slide':i,'part':spart,'showMasterSp':sroot.get('showMasterSp'),'shapes':top_shapes(sroot)})
    data={
      'pptx':str(pptx_path),
      'slide_size_emu':[int(pres.find('./p:sldSz',NS).get('cx')),int(pres.find('./p:sldSz',NS).get('cy'))],
      'ordered_layout_parts':ordered_layout_parts,
      'layout_index':3,
      'layout_part':layout_part,
      'layout_name':layout.get('name'),
      'layout_type':layout.get('type'),
      'layout_showMasterSp':layout.get('showMasterSp'),
      'layout_matchingName':layout.get('matchingName'),
      'layout_rels':lrels,
      'layout_shapes':top_shapes(layout),
      'slides_using_layout3':slides,
    }
    print(json.dumps(data,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))

function Get-EntryText([string]$name) {
  $entry = $zip.GetEntry($name)
  if ($null -eq $entry) { throw "Missing ZIP entry: $name" }
  $stream = $entry.Open()
  $reader = [System.IO.StreamReader]::new($stream, [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8)
  try { return $reader.ReadToEnd() } finally { $reader.Dispose(); $stream.Dispose() }
}
function Read-XmlPart([string]$name) {
  $doc = [System.Xml.XmlDocument]::new()
  $doc.PreserveWhitespace = $true
  $doc.LoadXml((Get-EntryText $name))
  return $doc
}
function New-Ns($doc) {
  $ns = [System.Xml.XmlNamespaceManager]::new($doc.NameTable)
  $ns.AddNamespace('"'p','http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/presentationml/2006/main')
  "'$ns.AddNamespace('"'a','http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/drawingml/2006/main')
  "'$ns.AddNamespace('"'r','http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships')
  "'$ns.AddNamespace('"'pr','http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/package/2006/relationships')
  return "'$ns
}
function Get-RelsPath([string]$part) {
  $slash = $part.LastIndexOf('"'/')
  "'$dir = if ($slash -ge 0) { $part.Substring(0,$slash) } else { '"'' }
  "'$file = if ($slash -ge 0) { $part.Substring($slash+1) } else { $part }
  return (($dir + '"'/_rels/' + "'$file + '"'.rels').TrimStart('/'))
}
function Resolve-Part([string]"'$part,[string]$target) {
  $base = [Uri]::new('"'http://pkg/' + "'$part)
  $resolved = [Uri]::new($base,$target)
  return $resolved.AbsolutePath.TrimStart('"'/')
}
function Read-Relationships([string]"'$part) {
  $doc = Read-XmlPart (Get-RelsPath $part)
  $ns = New-Ns $doc
  $map = @{}
  foreach ($rel in $doc.SelectNodes('"'/pr:Relationships/pr:Relationship',"'$ns)) {
    $map[$rel.Id] = [ordered]@{ type=($rel.Type -split '"'/')[-1]; target=(Resolve-Part "'$part $rel.Target) }
  }
  return $map
}
function Get-ShapeRows($doc) {
  $ns = New-Ns $doc
  $rows = @()
  $tree = $doc.SelectSingleNode('"'/*/p:cSld/p:spTree',"'$ns)
  foreach ($el in $tree.ChildNodes) {
    if ($el.LocalName -in @('"'nvGrpSpPr','grpSpPr')) { continue }
    "'$cnv = $el.SelectSingleNode('"'./p:nvSpPr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvPicPr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvGraphicFramePr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvCxnSpPr/p:cNvPr | ./p:nvGrpSpPr/p:cNvPr',"'$ns)
    $ph = $el.SelectSingleNode('"'.//p:nvPr/p:ph',"'$ns)
    $off = $el.SelectSingleNode('"'.//a:xfrm/a:off | .//p:xfrm/a:off',"'$ns)
    $ext = $el.SelectSingleNode('"'.//a:xfrm/a:ext | .//p:xfrm/a:ext',"'$ns)
    $textNodes = $el.SelectNodes('"'.//a:t',"'$ns)
    $txt = ($textNodes | ForEach-Object { $_.InnerText }) -join '"''
    "'$geom = $null
    if ($null -ne $off -and $null -ne $ext) {
      $vals = @([int64]$off.x,[int64]$off.y,[int64]$ext.cx,[int64]$ext.cy)
      $geom = [ordered]@{
        emu=$vals
        inches=@($vals | ForEach-Object { [math]::Round($_/914400,4) })
        points=@($vals | ForEach-Object { [math]::Round($_/12700,2) })
      }
    }
    $rows += [ordered]@{
      tag=$el.LocalName
      id=if($cnv){$cnv.id}else{$null}
      name=if($cnv){$cnv.name}else{$null}
      placeholder=if($ph){[ordered]@{type=$ph.type;idx=$ph.idx}}else{$null}
      geometry=$geom
      text=$txt
    }
  }
  return $rows
}

  $presPart='"'ppt/presentation.xml'
  "'$pres=Read-XmlPart $presPart

    $rid=$lid.GetAttribute('"'id','http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships')
    "'$layoutParts += $mrels[$rid].target
  }
  $layoutPart=$layoutParts[3]
  $layout=Read-XmlPart $layoutPart
  $lns=New-Ns $layout
  $lrels=Read-Relationships $layoutPart
  $slides=@()

  foreach($sid in $pres.SelectNodes('"'/p:presentation/p:sldIdLst/p:sldId',"'$pns)){
    $n++
    $rid=$sid.GetAttribute('"'id','http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships')
    "'$slidePart=$prels[$rid].target
    $srels=Read-Relationships $slidePart
    $slideLayoutRel=$srels.Values | Where-Object { $_.type -eq '"'slideLayout' } | Select-Object -First 1
    if("'$slideLayoutRel.target -eq $layoutPart){
      $sdoc=Read-XmlPart $slidePart
      $srows=Get-ShapeRows $sdoc
      $slides += [ordered]@{
        slide=$n
        part=$slidePart
        showMasterSp=$sdoc.DocumentElement.GetAttribute('"'showMasterSp')
        direct_shape_count="'$srows.Count
        direct_nonplaceholder_names=@($srows | Where-Object { $null -eq $_.placeholder } | ForEach-Object { $_.name })
      }
    }
  }
  $sldSz=$pres.SelectSingleNode('"'/p:presentation/p:sldSz',"'$pns)

    slide_size_emu=@([int64]$sldSz.cx,[int64]$sldSz.cy)

    layout_showMasterSp="'$layout.DocumentElement.GetAttribute('"'showMasterSp')
    layout_relationships="'$lrels
    layout_shapes=(Get-ShapeRows $layout)
    slides_using_layout3=$slides
  }
  $result | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 12
} finally {
  $zip.Dispose()

+# Codex Critic Review — HTML Visual Editor to PPTX Plan
+
+**VERDICT: REVISE.** The proposed architecture is sound, and the prior Q3 block on `add_slide()` can be lifted for this narrowly defined path: create a blank slide from Layout 3, retain only its inherited chrome, fill placeholder idx 10, and draw new content at absolute coordinates. However, `plan_review.md` should not be accepted unchanged. It reverses the identities of the two layout assets, misses a critical 23-existing-slide problem, and understates several preview/export fidelity risks.
+
+## 1. Layout chrome reliance versus slide cloning
+
+### Direct package finding
+
+For the current template SHA-256 `[REDACTED]`, zero-based `prs.slide_layouts[3]` maps to `ppt/slideLayouts/slideLayout4.xml`. Its layout shape tree directly contains:
+
+| Layout element | Actual role | Geometry in points | Relationship |
+|---|---|---:|---|
+| `그림 3` (`p:pic`, shape id 4) | Full-width gradient divider image | x 0.31, y 60.67, w 959.39, h 27.91 | `rId2` → `ppt/media/image5.jpeg` |
+| `그래픽 8` (`p:pic`, shape id 9) | Vieworks SVG logo | x 816.76, y 64.00, w 97.14, h 11.92 | SVG extension `rId3` → `ppt/media/image2.svg` |
+| `텍스트 개체 틀 2` | BODY placeholder idx 10 used as the title | x 45.55, y 26.75, w 725.29, h 37.25 | layout placeholder |
+| `슬라이드 번호 개체 틀 5` | Slide-number field/chrome | x 733.61, y 507.72, w 216.00, h 28.75 | regular layout shape |
+
+This corrects `plan_review.md`: **`그림 3` is the divider and `그래픽 8` is the logo, not the reverse.** The SVG content itself is the red Vieworks wordmark. The JPEG is 2934×85 and contains the visible gradient band. A pixel audit maps its visible non-white region to approximately x 46.41–788.68 pt and y 68.88–75.12 pt; its visual center is y 72.00 pt. The divider geometry was therefore not actually missing, although the JPEG's white padding makes the outer picture box an inaccurate proxy for the visible line.
+
+The exemplar slides using this layout do not contain duplicate slide-level copies of these two assets. Their slide-level shape trees contain their own freeform content plus the cloned idx-10 placeholder; the logo and divider are inherited from the layout. This is materially different from the exemplar compositions discussed in the Q3 review.
+
+### `add_slide()` behavior
+
+`python-pptx` implements `Slides.add_slide()` by creating a slide related to the selected layout and cloning the layout's eligible placeholders. BODY placeholders are cloneable; only date, footer, and slide-number placeholders are excluded. Consequently, idx 10 should be available as `slide.placeholders[10]`, while the logo/divider remain layout-level shapes rather than copies in `slide.shapes`. See the official [`pptx.slide` source](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/slide.html) and [placeholder inheritance explanation](https://github.com/scanny/python-pptx/blob/master/docs/user/placeholders-understanding.rst).
+
+Using `add_slide(layout_3)` therefore brings in the chrome at its native layout coordinates without rescaling or geometric distortion, provided the slide is added to this same template package and master-graphics inheritance is not disabled. Slide cloning is unnecessary for this path.
+
+The old Q3 block is still valid for a different claim: `add_slide(layout_3)` does **not** reproduce any exemplar slide's freeform diagram, table, or card composition. It is safe only because this new plan deliberately draws its own body content.
+
+### Required reliability checks
+
+The final plan must require a smoke test that adds one slide, saves, reopens, and renders it in the target PowerPoint/conversion environment. Assert that:
+
+1. the new slide still relates to `slideLayout4.xml`;
+2. the layout still relates to both `image5.jpeg` and `image2.svg`;
+3. placeholder idx 10 exists and can be filled;
+4. the logo and divider are visible and undistorted in the rendered output; and
+5. the SVG logo survives any LibreOffice or server-side conversion path actually used.
+
+## 2. Missing findings and corrections
+
+### Critical — the source template already contains 23 slides
+
+`Presentation("Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx")` followed by `add_slide()` appends a 24th slide. It does not produce a one-slide export. This would ship all exemplar slides and make the inherited page number wrong. The public `Slides` API exposes addition but no supported deletion operation.
+
+The backend must use a separately verified **clean renderer template** that retains the master/layout/media parts but contains no exemplar slides, or define and test a deterministic package-preparation step before renderer development. Do not make private slide-deletion code part of each export request. Never overwrite the source template.
+
+### Critical — layout selection must fail closed
+
+Hard-coding layout index `3` is acceptable only while the template hash is pinned. The JSON/backend contract should include the expected template or manifest version, and startup should verify the SHA plus a layout signature: idx 10, slide size 12192000×6858000 EMU, and the two required layout media relationships. A changed or reordered template must fail with a clear error rather than silently selecting a different layout.
+
+### High — inherited chrome is invisible to `slide.shapes` and always behind slide content
+
+Collision checks that inspect only `slide.shapes` will not see the logo, divider, or page-number chrome. New slide-level shapes can cover them, and layout shapes cannot simply be brought above slide content. Maintain explicit locked/protected regions in the canvas manifest. At minimum, keep user elements below the header boundary and away from the footer/page-number region.
+
+The editor's red boundary must remain editor-only CSS/SVG chrome and must not be serializable as a content element. Based on the current asset, a reasonable Phase-1 assumption is a thin `#FF0032` line from about x 46.4 to 788.7 at y 72.0 logical units. Record that as an approximation of the gradient's visible center, not as the divider picture's outer box.
+
+### High — title access and styling must be explicit
+
+Layout 3 has no semantic TITLE placeholder, so `slide.shapes.title` remains `None`. Use `slide.placeholders[10]` by placeholder idx, not by collection position. After assigning text, explicitly set all title run/paragraph formatting required by the product; do not assume the BODY placeholder's inherited style equals the editor's Century Gothic default.
+
+### High — 960×540 is exact, but the browser contract is not yet complete
+
+The factor `1 logical unit = 1 pt = 12,700 EMU` is exact for this 12192000×6858000 EMU slide. Preserve that benefit, but add these rules:
+
+- Store and serialize only 960×540 logical coordinates. DOM client pixels, responsive widths, zoomed `getBoundingClientRect()` values, and `devicePixelRatio` must never enter the IR. Pointer coordinates must be transformed back through the single stage scale.
+- Define `x/y/w/h` as the shape's unrotated outer geometry. Use center-origin rotation in both HTML and PowerPoint.
+- Define whether stroke is included in `w/h`. CSS `box-sizing`, inward CSS borders, and PowerPoint's centered line stroke otherwise produce visible edge drift. Include stroke-width cases in the calibration slide.
+- Clamp all geometry to the slide and protected regions, reject non-positive dimensions, and round only at the final `logical × 12700` conversion.
+
+### High — browser text is a preview, not proof of PowerPoint fit
+
+The visual editor reduces text-overflow risk but does not eliminate it. Browser and PowerPoint can differ in font fallback, kerning, line breaking, paragraph spacing, and vertical metrics. The renderer and CSS must explicitly align:
+
+- left/right/top/bottom margins;
+- word wrap and no-auto-resize behavior;
+- horizontal and vertical alignment;
+- paragraph `space_before`, `space_after`, and line spacing/line-height;
+- font family, size, weight, and mixed Korean/Latin fallback; and
+- normalization of `contenteditable` markup (`div`, `br`, non-breaking spaces) into explicit paragraphs, breaks, and runs.
+
+Century Gothic should be treated as the Latin default. Use an explicit installed Hangul fallback in both CSS and DrawingML (`a:ea`), and detect/warn when Century Gothic is unavailable in the browser or export environment. `python-pptx`'s public `Font.name` does not by itself establish the East-Asian font.
+
+`plan_review.md` also says "there is no autofit" too absolutely. [`TextFrame.fit_text()` exists](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/text.html), but it is not a PowerPoint rendering engine and is a poor foundation for mixed-font CJK fidelity. The plan should say **no guaranteed headless PowerPoint-equivalent autofit** and keep deterministic render QA as the authority.
+
+The claim that XML-illegal controls necessarily corrupt output is also overstated for `python-pptx` 1.0.x: its text API escapes unsupported control characters. Input sanitization is still sensible for interoperability, but it should be described as a validation policy, not as an inevitable corruption fix.
+
+### Medium — shape and connector defaults must never leak through
+
+The previous analysis correctly identified theme-blue fills/effects. The implementation rules should require every generated shape to set fill, line fill/color/width, shadow/effects, text color, fonts, margins, wrapping, and alignment explicitly. Use opaque `#RRGGBB` in Phase 1 unless transparency semantics are separately specified. New content defaults to `#FF0032`; inherited template chrome keeps its native branding.
+
+Flowcharts also need a connector/arrow primitive. Decide it in the Phase-1 schema rather than after the toolbar is built. If included, define endpoints in logical units, render connectors behind nodes, add arrowheads explicitly in DrawingML, and treat shape gluing as optional/tested behavior. `begin_connect()`/`end_connect()` are experimental and should not be assumed reliable for arbitrary triangles or connection-site indices. Save, reopen, and render every connector fixture.
+
+## 3. Rules the main worker should carry into the final plan
+
+1. **Adopt Layout 3 chrome; do not clone exemplar slides** for the visual-editor path.
+2. **Correct the asset map:** `그림 3`/`image5.jpeg` is the divider; `그래픽 8`/`image2.svg` is the logo.
+3. **Create and verify a clean renderer template before feature work** so exports do not include the 23 exemplars.
+4. **Pin the template SHA and layout signature** and fail closed on any mismatch.
+5. **Fill BODY placeholder idx 10 directly**; never use `slide.shapes.title` for this layout.
+6. **Keep editor chrome out of Canvas IR** and lock header/footer exclusion zones.
+7. **Keep the 960×540 logical model**, with a single exact 12,700-EMU conversion and explicit zoom inversion.
+8. **Specify border, rotation, text-margin, paragraph, fallback-font, and contenteditable normalization semantics** before implementing serialization.
+9. **Make all PPTX styling explicit**, including removing inherited Office effects and setting both Latin and East-Asian fonts.
+10. **Add a connector schema/tool or explicitly declare arrows out of scope.** Do not claim "flowchart" support with nodes only.
+11. **Run calibration and round-trip QA first:** corners, strokes, rotated shapes, known text wraps, mixed KO/EN, logo/divider visibility, save/reopen, and render comparison.
+12. **Do not claim WYSIWYG from coordinate equality alone.** Acceptance should use tolerances and rendered evidence from the actual target consumer.
+
+## Assumptions and caveats
+
+- This review is based on read-only OPC/XML and media inspection of the exact template hash above. No deck was modified or generated.
+- No local PowerPoint or LibreOffice executable was available for an end-to-end render, so visual inheritance is structurally proven but still requires the smoke test described above.
+- The suggested editor-line geometry is an evidence-based approximation of the visible pixels in the current gradient JPEG. The final plan may choose a simpler line treatment as long as it remains non-exportable and the approximation is documented.

+`python-pptx` implements `Slides.add_slide()` by creating a slide related to the selected layout and cloning the layout's eligible placeholders. BODY placeholders are cloneable; only date, footer, and slide-number placeholders are excluded. Consequently, idx 10 should be available as `slide.placeholders[10]`, while the logo/divider remain layout-level shapes rather than copies in `slide.shapes`. See the official [`pptx.slide` source](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/slide.html) and [placeholder inheritance explanation](https://github.com/scanny/python-pptx/blob/master/docs/user/placeholders-understanding.rst).
+
+Using `add_slide(layout_3)` therefore brings in the chrome at its native layout coordinates without rescaling or geometric distortion, provided the slide is added to this same template package and master-graphics inheritance is not disabled. Slide cloning is unnecessary for this path.
+
+The old Q3 block is still valid for a different claim: `add_slide(layout_3)` does **not** reproduce any exemplar slide's freeform diagram, table, or card composition. It is safe only because this new plan deliberately draws its own body content.
+
+### Required reliability checks
+
+The final plan must require a smoke test that adds one slide, saves, reopens, and renders it in the target PowerPoint/conversion environment. Assert that:
+
+1. the new slide still relates to `slideLayout4.xml`;
+2. the layout still relates to both `image5.jpeg` and `image2.svg`;
+3. placeholder idx 10 exists and can be filled;
+4. the logo and divider are visible and undistorted in the rendered output; and
+5. the SVG logo survives any LibreOffice or server-side conversion path actually used.
+
+## 2. Missing findings and corrections
+
+### Critical — the source template already contains 23 slides
+
+`Presentation("Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx")` followed by `add_slide()` appends a 24th slide. It does not produce a one-slide export. This would ship all exemplar slides and make the inherited page number wrong. The public `Slides` API exposes addition but no supported deletion operation.
+
+The backend must use a separately verified **clean renderer template** that retains the master/layout/media parts but contains no exemplar slides, or define and test a deterministic package-preparation step before renderer development. Do not make private slide-deletion code part of each export request. Never overwrite the source template.
+
+### Critical — layout selection must fail closed
+
+Hard-coding layout index `3` is acceptable only while the template hash is pinned. The JSON/backend contract should include the expected template or manifest version, and startup should verify the SHA plus a layout signature: idx 10, slide size 12192000×6858000 EMU, and the two required layout media relationships. A changed or reordered template must fail with a clear error rather than silently selecting a different layout.
+
+### High — inherited chrome is invisible to `slide.shapes` and always behind slide content
+
+Collision checks that inspect only `slide.shapes` will not see the logo, divider, or page-number chrome. New slide-level shapes can cover them, and layout shapes cannot simply be brought above slide content. Maintain explicit locked/protected regions in the canvas manifest. At minimum, keep user elements below the header boundary and away from the footer/page-number region.
+
+The editor's red boundary must remain editor-only CSS/SVG chrome and must not be serializable as a content element. Based on the current asset, a reasonable Phase-1 assumption is a thin `#FF0032` line from about x 46.4 to 788.7 at y 72.0 logical units. Record that as an approximation of the gradient's visible center, not as the divider picture's outer box.
+
+### High — title access and styling must be explicit
+
+Layout 3 has no semantic TITLE placeholder, so `slide.shapes.title` remains `None`. Use `slide.placeholders[10]` by placeholder idx, not by collection position. After assigning text, explicitly set all title run/paragraph formatting required by the product; do not assume the BODY placeholder's inherited style equals the editor's Century Gothic default.
+
+### High — 960×540 is exact, but the browser contract is not yet complete
+
+The factor `1 logical unit = 1 pt = 12,700 EMU` is exact for this 12192000×6858000 EMU slide. Preserve that benefit, but add these rules:
+
+- Store and serialize only 960×540 logical coordinates. DOM client pixels, responsive widths, zoomed `getBoundingClientRect()` values, and `devicePixelRatio` must never enter the IR. Pointer coordinates must be transformed back through the single stage scale.
+- Define `x/y/w/h` as the shape's unrotated outer geometry. Use center-origin rotation in both HTML and PowerPoint.
+- Define whether stroke is included in `w/h`. CSS `box-sizing`, inward CSS borders, and PowerPoint's centered line stroke otherwise produce visible edge drift. Include stroke-width cases in the calibration slide.
+- Clamp all geometry to the slide and protected regions, reject non-positive dimensions, and round only at the final `logical × 12700` conversion.
+
+### High — browser text is a preview, not proof of PowerPoint fit
+
+The visual editor reduces text-overflow risk but does not eliminate it. Browser and PowerPoint can differ in font fallback, kerning, line breaking, paragraph spacing, and vertical metrics. The renderer and CSS must explicitly align:
+
+- left/right/top/bottom margins;
+- word wrap and no-auto-resize behavior;
+- horizontal and vertical alignment;
+- paragraph `space_before`, `space_after`, and line spacing/line-height;
+- font family, size, weight, and mixed Korean/Latin fallback; and
+- normalization of `contenteditable` markup (`div`, `br`, non-breaking spaces) into explicit paragraphs, breaks, and runs.
+
+Century Gothic should be treated as the Latin default. Use an explicit installed Hangul fallback in both CSS and DrawingML (`a:ea`), and detect/warn when Century Gothic is unavailable in the browser or export environment. `python-pptx`'s public `Font.name` does not by itself establish the East-Asian font.
+
+`plan_review.md` also says "there is no autofit" too absolutely. [`TextFrame.fit_text()` exists](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/text.html), but it is not a PowerPoint rendering engine and is a poor foundation for mixed-font CJK fidelity. The plan should say **no guaranteed headless PowerPoint-equivalent autofit** and keep deterministic render QA as the authority.
+
+The claim that XML-illegal controls necessarily corrupt output is also overstated for `python-pptx` 1.0.x: its text API escapes unsupported control characters. Input sanitization is still sensible for interoperability, but it should be described as a validation policy, not as an inevitable corruption fix.
+
+### Medium — shape and connector defaults must never leak through
+
+The previous analysis correctly identified theme-blue fills/effects. The implementation rules should require every generated shape to set fill, line fill/color/width, shadow/effects, text color, fonts, margins, wrapping, and alignment explicitly. Use opaque `#RRGGBB` in Phase 1 unless transparency semantics are separately specified. New content defaults to `#FF0032`; inherited template chrome keeps its native branding.
+
+Flowcharts also need a connector/arrow primitive. Decide it in the Phase-1 schema rather than after the toolbar is built. If included, define endpoints in logical units, render connectors behind nodes, add arrowheads explicitly in DrawingML, and treat shape gluing as optional/tested behavior. `begin_connect()`/`end_connect()` are experimental and should not be assumed reliable for arbitrary triangles or connection-site indices. Save, reopen, and render every connector fixture.
+
+## 3. Rules the main worker should carry into the final plan
+
+1. **Adopt Layout 3 chrome; do not clone exemplar slides** for the visual-editor path.
+2. **Correct the asset map:** `그림 3`/`image5.jpeg` is the divider; `그래픽 8`/`image2.svg` is the logo.
+3. **Create and verify a clean renderer template before feature work** so exports do not include the 23 exemplars.
+4. **Pin the template SHA and layout signature** and fail closed on any mismatch.
+5. **Fill BODY placeholder idx 10 directly**; never use `slide.shapes.title` for this layout.
+6. **Keep editor chrome out of Canvas IR** and lock header/footer exclusion zones.
+7. **Keep the 960×540 logical model**, with a single exact 12,700-EMU conversion and explicit zoom inversion.
+8. **Specify border, rotation, text-margin, paragraph, fallback-font, and contenteditable normalization semantics** before implementing serialization.
+9. **Make all PPTX styling explicit**, including removing inherited Office effects and setting both Latin and East-Asian fonts.
+10. **Add a connector schema/tool or explicitly declare arrows out of scope.** Do not claim "flowchart" support with nodes only.
+11. **Run calibration and round-trip QA first:** corners, strokes, rotated shapes, known text wraps, mixed KO/EN, logo/divider visibility, save/reopen, and render comparison.
+12. **Do not claim WYSIWYG from coordinate equality alone.** Acceptance should use tolerances and rendered evidence from the actual target consumer.
+
+## Assumptions and caveats
+
+- This review is based on read-only OPC/XML and media inspection of the exact template hash above. No deck was modified or generated.
+- No local PowerPoint or LibreOffice executable was available for an end-to-end render, so visual inheritance is structurally proven but still requires the smoke test described above.
+- The suggested editor-line geometry is an evidence-based approximation of the visible pixels in the current gradient JPEG. The final plan may choose a simpler line treatment as long as it remains non-exportable and the approximation is documented.

+# Codex Critic Review — HTML Visual Editor to PPTX Plan
+
+**VERDICT: REVISE.** The proposed architecture is sound, and the prior Q3 block on `add_slide()` can be lifted for this narrowly defined path: create a blank slide from Layout 3, retain only its inherited chrome, fill placeholder idx 10, and draw new content at absolute coordinates. However, `plan_review.md` should not be accepted unchanged. It reverses the identities of the two layout assets, misses a critical 23-existing-slide problem, and understates several preview/export fidelity risks.
+
+## 1. Layout chrome reliance versus slide cloning
+
+### Direct package finding
+
+For the current template SHA-256 `[REDACTED]`, zero-based `prs.slide_layouts[3]` maps to `ppt/slideLayouts/slideLayout4.xml`. Its layout shape tree directly contains:
+
+| Layout element | Actual role | Geometry in points | Relationship |
+|---|---|---:|---|
+| `그림 3` (`p:pic`, shape id 4) | Full-width gradient divider image | x 0.31, y 60.67, w 959.39, h 27.91 | `rId2` → `ppt/media/image5.jpeg` |
+| `그래픽 8` (`p:pic`, shape id 9) | Vieworks SVG logo | x 816.76, y 64.00, w 97.14, h 11.92 | SVG extension `rId3` → `ppt/media/image2.svg` |
+| `텍스트 개체 틀 2` | BODY placeholder idx 10 used as the title | x 45.55, y 26.75, w 725.29, h 37.25 | layout placeholder |
+| `슬라이드 번호 개체 틀 5` | Slide-number field/chrome | x 733.61, y 507.72, w 216.00, h 28.75 | regular layout shape |
+
+This corrects `plan_review.md`: **`그림 3` is the divider and `그래픽 8` is the logo, not the reverse.** The SVG content itself is the red Vieworks wordmark. The JPEG is 2934×85 and contains the visible gradient band. A pixel audit maps its visible non-white region to approximately x 46.41–788.68 pt and y 68.88–75.12 pt; its visual center is y 72.00 pt. The divider geometry was therefore not actually missing, although the JPEG's white padding makes the outer picture box an inaccurate proxy for the visible line.
+
+The exemplar slides using this layout do not contain duplicate slide-level copies of these two assets. Their slide-level shape trees contain their own freeform content plus the cloned idx-10 placeholder; the logo and divider are inherited from the layout. +
+The exemplar slides using this layout do not contain duplicate slide-level copies of these two assets. Their slide-level shape trees contain their own freeform content plus the cloned idx-10 placeholder; the logo and divider are inherited from the layout. This is materially different from the exemplar compositions discussed in the Q3 review.
+
+### `add_slide()` behavior
+
+`python-pptx` implements `Slides.add_slide()` by creating a slide related to the selected layout and cloning the layout's eligible placeholders. BODY placeholders are cloneable; only date, footer, and slide-number placeholders are excluded. Consequently, idx 10 should be available as `slide.placeholders[10]`, while the logo/divider remain layout-level shapes rather than copies in `slide.shapes`. See the official [`pptx.slide` source](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/slide.html) and [placeholder inheritance explanation](https://github.com/scanny/python-pptx/blob/master/docs/user/placeholders-understanding.rst).
+
+Using `add_slide(layout_3)` therefore brings in the chrome at its native layout coordinates without rescaling or geometric distortion, provided the slide is added to this same template package and master-graphics inheritance is not disabled. Slide cloning is unnecessary for this path.
+
+The old Q3 block is still valid for a different claim: `add_slide(layout_3)` does **not** reproduce any exemplar slide's freeform diagram, table, or card composition. It is safe only because this new plan deliberately draws its own body content.
+
+### Required reliability checks
+
+The final plan must require a smoke test that adds one slide, saves, reopens, and renders it in the target PowerPoint/conversion environment. Assert that:
+
+1. the new slide still relates to `slideLayout4.xml`;
+2. the layout still relates to both `image5.jpeg` and `image2.svg`;
+3. placeholder idx 10 exists and can be filled;
+4. the logo and divider are visible and undistorted in the rendered output; and
+5. the SVG logo survives any LibreOffice or server-side conversion path actually used.
+
+## 2. Missing findings and corrections
+
+### Critical — the source template already contains 23 slides
+
+`Presentation("Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx")` followed by `add_slide()` appends a 24th slide. It does not produce a one-slide export. This would ship all exemplar slides and make the inherited page number wrong. The public `Slides` API exposes addition but no supported deletion operation.
+
+The backend must use a separately verified **clean renderer template** that retains the master/layout/media parts but contains no exemplar slides, or define and test a deterministic package-preparation step before renderer development. Do not make private slide-deletion code part of each export request. Never overwrite the source template.
+
+### Critical — layout selection must fail closed
+
+Hard-coding layout index `3` is acceptable only while the template hash is pinned. The JSON/backend contract should include the expected template or manifest version, and startup should verify the SHA plus a layout signature: idx 10, slide size 12192000×6858000 EMU, and the two required layout media relationships. A changed or reordered template must fail with a clear error rather than silently selecting a different layout.
+
+### High — inherited chrome is invisible to `slide.shapes` and always behind slide content
+
+Collision checks that inspect only `slide.shapes` will not see the logo, divider, or page-number chrome. New slide-level shapes can cover them, and layout shapes cannot simply be brought above slide content. Maintain explicit locked/protected regions in the canvas manifest. At minimum, keep user elements below the header boundary and away from the footer/page-number region.
+
+The editor's red boundary must remain editor-only CSS/SVG chrome and must not be serializable as a content element. Based on the current asset, a reasonable Phase-1 assumption is a thin `#FF0032` line from about x 46.4 to 788.7 at y 72.0 logical units. Record that as an approximation of the gradient's visible center, not as the divider picture's outer box.
+
+### High — title access and styling must be explicit
+
+Layout 3 has no semantic TITLE placeholder, so `slide.shapes.title` remains `None`. Use `slide.placeholders[10]` by placeholder idx, not by collection position. After assigning text, explicitly set all title run/paragraph formatting required by the product; do not assume the BODY placeholder's inherited style equals the editor's Century Gothic default.
+
+### High — 960×540 is exact, but the browser contract is not yet complete
+
+The factor `1 logical unit = 1 pt = 12,700 EMU` is exact for this 12192000×6858000 EMU slide. Preserve that benefit, but add these rules:
+
+- Store and serialize only 960×540 logical coordinates. DOM client pixels, responsive widths, zoomed `getBoundingClientRect()` values, and `devicePixelRatio` must never enter the IR. Pointer coordinates must be transformed back through the single stage scale.
+- Define `x/y/w/h` as the shape's unrotated outer geometry. Use center-origin rotation in both HTML and PowerPoint.
+- Define whether stroke is included in `w/h`. CSS `box-sizing`, inward CSS borders, and PowerPoint's centered line stroke otherwise produce visible edge drift. Include stroke-width cases in the calibration slide.
+- Clamp all geometry to the slide and protected regions, reject non-positive dimensions, and round only at the final `logical × 12700` conversion.
+
+### High — browser text is a preview, not proof of PowerPoint fit
+
+The visual editor reduces text-overflow risk but does not eliminate it. Browser and PowerPoint can differ in font fallback, kerning, line breaking, paragraph spacing, and vertical metrics. The renderer and CSS must explicitly align:
+
+- left/right/top/bottom margins;
+- word wrap and no-auto-resize behavior;
+- horizontal and vertical alignment;
+- paragraph `space_before`, `space_after`, and line spacing/line-height;
+- font family, size, weight, and mixed Korean/Latin fallback; and
+- normalization of `contenteditable` markup (`div`, `br`, non-breaking spaces) into explicit paragraphs, breaks, and runs.
+
+Century Gothic should be treated as the Latin default. Use an explicit installed Hangul fallback in both CSS and DrawingML (`a:ea`), and detect/warn when Century Gothic is unavailable in the browser or export environment. `python-pptx`'s public `Font.name` does not by itself establish the East-Asian font.
+
+`plan_review.md` also says "there is no autofit" too absolutely. [`TextFrame.fit_text()` exists](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/text.html), but it is not a PowerPoint rendering engine and is a poor foundation for mixed-font CJK fidelity. The plan should say **no guaranteed headless PowerPoint-equivalent autofit** and keep deterministic render QA as the authority.
+
+The claim that XML-illegal controls necessarily corrupt output is also overstated for `python-pptx` 1.0.x: its text API escapes unsupported control characters. Input sanitization is still sensible for interoperability, but it should be described as a validation policy, not as an inevitable corruption fix.
+
+### Medium — shape and connector defaults must never leak through
+
+The previous analysis correctly identified theme-blue fills/effects. The implementation rules should require every generated shape to set fill, line fill/color/width, shadow/effects, text color, fonts, margins, wrapping, and alignment explicitly. Use opaque `#RRGGBB` in Phase 1 unless transparency semantics are separately specified. New content defaults to `#FF0032`; inherited template chrome keeps its native branding.
+
+Flowcharts also need a connector/arrow primitive. Decide it in the Phase-1 schema rather than after the toolbar is built. If included, define endpoints in logical units, render connectors behind nodes, add arrowheads explicitly in DrawingML, and treat shape gluing as optional/tested behavior. `begin_connect()`/`end_connect()` are experimental and should not be assumed reliable for arbitrary triangles or connection-site indices. Save, reopen, and render every connector fixture.
+
+## 3. Rules the main worker should carry into the final plan
+
+1. **Adopt Layout 3 chrome; do not clone exemplar slides** for the visual-editor path.
+2. **Correct the asset map:** `그림 3`/`image5.jpeg` is the divider; `그래픽 8`/`image2.svg` is the logo.
+3. **Create and verify a clean renderer template before feature work** so exports do not include the 23 exemplars.
+4. **Pin the template SHA and layout signature** and fail closed on any mismatch.
+5. **Fill BODY placeholder idx 10 directly**; never use `slide.shapes.title` for this layout.
+6. **Keep editor chrome out of Canvas IR** and lock header/footer exclusion zones.
+7. **Keep the 960×540 logical model**, with a single exact 12,700-EMU conversion and explicit zoom inversion.
+8. **Specify border, rotation, text-margin, paragraph, fallback-font, and contenteditable normalization semantics** before implementing serialization.
+9. **Make all PPTX styling explicit**, including removing inherited Office effects and setting both Latin and East-Asian fonts.
+10. **Add a connector schema/tool or explicitly declare arrows out of scope.** Do not claim "flowchart" support with nodes only.
+11. **Run calibration and round-trip QA first:** corners, strokes, rotated shapes, known text wraps, mixed KO/EN, logo/divider visibility, save/reopen, and render comparison.
+12. **Do not claim WYSIWYG from coordinate equality alone.** Acceptance should use tolerances and rendered evidence from the actual target consumer.
+
+## Assumptions and caveats
+
+- This review is based on read-only OPC/XML and media inspection of the exact template hash above. No deck was modified or generated.
+- No local PowerPoint or LibreOffice executable was available for an end-to-end render, so visual inheritance is structurally proven but still requires the smoke test described above.
+- The suggested editor-line geometry is an evidence-based approximation of the visible pixels in the current gradient JPEG. The final plan may choose a simpler line treatment as long as it remains non-exportable and the approximation is documented.
